A.2.1.
Convertibility

\frac{~}{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{unit}}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{unit}}}\frac{~}{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{bool}}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{bool}}}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}B}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\sigma}}{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\times}}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}B}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\times}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\sigma}}

A.3.
Semantics of types

A.3.1.
Types

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle:\text{finite discrete measurable space}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{unit}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\text{the one-point space $\{\star\}$}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{bool}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\{\top,\bot\}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\times}}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}B}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\times\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}B}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle:\text{measurable space}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{unit}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\text{the one-point space $\{\star\}$}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{bool}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\text{the discrete two-point space $\{\top,\bot\}$}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{real}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\sigma}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\times}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\sigma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\times\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

A.3.2.
Contexts

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle:\text{measurable space}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\prod_{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x}\in\operatorname{dom}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}}\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x})\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket:\text{finite discrete measurable space}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket=\prod_{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X}\in\operatorname{dom}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}}\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}({\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X})\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

A.3.3.
Convertibility

Lemma A.1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}
then
\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\mathrel{\leftrightsquigarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}
. ∎

A.4.
Semantics of pure programs

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!

\displaystyle:\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\delta({\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X})

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{true}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\top

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{false}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\bot

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{\land}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\top,&\text{if }\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)=\top\\
\bot,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{\neg}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\bot,&\text{if }\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)=\top\\
\top,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\langle\rangle}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\star

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M},{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\rangle}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta),\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta))

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{fst}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta))

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{snd}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta)

\displaystyle=\pi_{2}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\delta))

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}:{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!

\displaystyle:\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\rm measurable}{\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\gamma({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x})

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{true}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\top

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{false}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\bot

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003ptr\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=r

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}+}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)+\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}-}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=-\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\cdot}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\cdot\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\leq}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\top,&\text{if }\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\leq\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\\
\bot,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}()}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\star

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\right)}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma),\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{fst}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{snd}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\pi_{2}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))

A.5.
Effectful programs: high-level model

Definition A.2.

Let
\mathrm{Dist}
be the distribution monad defined over measurable spaces.
Let
\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}
be the writer monad transformer applied
to
\mathrm{Dist}
and the monoid
([0,1],1)
of nonegative reals under multiplication.
Concretely,
\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}
sends a measurable space
A

to the set
\mathrm{Dist}([0,1]\times A)
.
Let
\mathrm{ret}~x

and
x\leftarrow\mu;f(x)
denote the usual monad operations with respect to
\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}
,
and let
\overline{(\cdot)}:\mathrm{Dist}(A)\to\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}(A)
be the usual lifting operation.
In addition to the usual operations,

•

Let
\mathrm{score}:[0,1]\to\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}\{\star\}
be the
function that sends a weight
w
to the Dirac
distribution
\delta_{(w,\star)}
centered at
(w,\star)
.

Definition A.3.

For
p\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
\mathrm{flip}(p)

be the Bernoulli distribution with parameter
p

if
p\in[0,1]
,
and a point mass at
\bot
otherwise.

Definition A.4.

For
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
\operatorname{uniform}(a,b)

be the uniform distribution on the interval
[a,b]

if
a\leq b
,
and a point mass at
\min(a,b)
otherwise.

Definition A.5.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
\operatorname{poisson}(\lambda)

be the Poisson distribution with parameter
\lambda

if
\lambda>0
,
and a point mass at
0
otherwise.

A.5.1.
Exact sublanguage

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle:\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\times\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\to\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}{\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{flip}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{poisson}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle=\overline{\operatorname{poisson}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{flip}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{score}\left(\operatorname{flip}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{poisson}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{score}\left(\operatorname{poisson}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{uniform}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{score}\left(\operatorname{uniform}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma),\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\right)

A.6.
Effectful programs: low-level model

Definition A.6.

A
finite conditional probability space

is a triple
(\Omega,\mu,E)

where
(1)
\Omega
is a nonempty finite prefix of
\mathbb{N}
,
(2)
\mu:\Omega\to[0,1]
is a discrete probability distribution,
and (3)
E
is a subset of
\Omega
.
Let
\mathrm{FCPS}
be the set of finite conditional probability spaces.

For readability, finite conditional probability spaces

(\Omega,\mu,E)
will be written
\Omega
unless disambiguation is needed.

Lemma A.7.

\mathrm{FCPS}
is a measurable space.

Proof.

There is an injective function

i:\mathrm{FCPS}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{N}\times\mathrm{list}(\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{P}_{\rm fin}\mathbb{N}

sending a finite conditional probability space

(\{0,\dots,n-1\},\mu,E)

to the triple

(n,[\mu(0),\dots,\mu(n-1)],E)
.
The codomain of
i
is a measurable space with
\sigma
-algebra defined in the
standard way, and
the image of
i
is a measurable subset of this space.
The injection
i
identifies the image of
i
with
\mathrm{FCPS}
,
making
\mathrm{FCPS}
a measurable space by taking preimages along
i
.
∎

Definition A.8.

A
map of finite conditional probability spaces

f:(\Omega,\mu,E)\to(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})

is a measure-preserving map
f:(\Omega,\mu)\to(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})

such that
E\subseteq f^{-1}(E^{\prime})
.
For two finite conditional probability spaces

(\Omega,\mu,E)
and
(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})
,
let
(\Omega,\mu,E)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})

be the set of maps from

(\Omega,\mu,E)
to
(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})
.

Definition A.9.

For every
{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}

let
\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}}
and
\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}}
be the following

\mathrm{FCPS}
-indexed families of sets:

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}}

\displaystyle:\mathrm{FCPS}\to\mathbf{Set}

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}}\Omega

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}\left(\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}\Omega)\times(\Omega^{\prime}\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)\right)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}}

\displaystyle:\mathrm{FCPS}\to\mathbf{Set}

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}}\Omega

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}\left(\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}\Omega)\times\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\right)

Proof.

For these to be well-defined, the arguments to
\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}

must be measurable spaces.
Elements of

\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}\Omega)\times(\Omega^{\prime}\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)

are triples
(\Omega^{\prime},f,X)
where
\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}
and
f
is a map of
finite conditional probability spaces and
X:\Omega^{\prime}\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket
.
There is a canonical injective function
i
from this set to the set

\mathrm{FCPS}\times(\mathbb{N}\xrightarrow{\rm fin}\mathbb{N})\times(\mathbb{N}\xrightarrow{\rm fin}\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)

whose elements are triples
(\Omega^{\prime},f,X)

where
\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}
and
f,X
are partial functions of type
\mathbb{N}\rightharpoonup\mathbb{N}

and
\mathbb{N}\rightharpoonup\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket
respectively with finite domain.
This set is a measurable space by
Lemma

A.7

and by putting the discrete
\sigma
-algebras on

\mathbb{N}\xrightarrow{\rm fin}\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbb{N}\xrightarrow{\rm fin}\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket
.
The image of
i
is a measurable subset of this space,
making

\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}\Omega)\times(\Omega^{\prime}\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)

a measurable space too.
The analogous argument also makes

\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathrm{FCPS}}(\Omega^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FCPS}}\Omega)\times\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

into a measurable space.
∎

Definition A.10.

For two finite conditional probability spaces

(\Omega,\mu,E)

and

(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})
,
their
tensor product
,
written
(\Omega,\mu,E)\otimes(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})
,
is

(f(\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime}),\nu\circ f^{-1},f(E\times E^{\prime}))

where
\nu:\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime}\to[0,1]
is the measure

\nu(\omega,\omega^{\prime})=\mu(\omega)\mu^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime})

and
f
is an arbitrary isomorphism
\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime}\to\{1,\dots,|\Omega||\Omega^{\prime}|\}

(such as
f(\omega,\omega^{\prime})=|\Omega^{\prime}|\omega+\omega^{\prime}
).
There are canonical projection maps

\pi_{1}:(\Omega,\mu,E)\otimes(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to(\Omega,\mu,E)\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{2}:(\Omega,\mu,E)\otimes(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to(\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}).

The tensor product has a unit, written
\mathrm{emp}
,
defined as
(\Omega_{\mathrm{emp}},\mu_{\mathrm{emp}},E_{\mathrm{emp}})

where
\Omega_{\mathrm{emp}}=E_{\mathrm{emp}}=\{0\}

and
\mu(0)=1
.

Definition A.11.

To model sampling from finite conditional probability spaces,

•

For any nonempty
set
\Omega
, let
\mathrm{zero}_{\Omega}:\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}(A)
be the Dirac
distribution at
(0,\nu):[0,1]\times\Omega
where
\nu
is an
arbitrary element of
\Omega
.

•

For any finite conditional probability
space
(\Omega,\mu,E)
, let
\mu|_{E}:\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}(\Omega)

be
\mathrm{zero}_{\Omega}
if
\mu(E)=0
, and the lifting of the
conditional distribution of
\mu
given
E
if
\mu(E)>0
.

A.6.1.
Exact sublanguage

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)

\displaystyle:\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\times(\Omega\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)\to\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}}}{(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{ret}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\circ D)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{let}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{be}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{in}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &(\Omega_{1},f_{1},X)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D);\\
&(\Omega_{2},f_{2},Y)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega_{1})(\gamma,(D\circ f_{1})[{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}X}\mapsto X]);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega_{2},f_{1}\circ f_{2},Y)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{if}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{then}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{else}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{if}~\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\\
&\mathrm{then}~\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)\\
&\mathrm{else}~\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}N}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{flip}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &p:=\mathrm{if}~\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\in[0,1]~\mathrm{then}~\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)~\mathrm{else}~0;\\
&\Omega_{\rm flip}:=(\{0,1\},\mu,\{0,1\})\text{ where $\mu(1)=p$};\\
&\Omega^{\prime}:=\Omega\otimes\Omega_{\rm flip};\\
&X:=\omega^{\prime}\mapsto\mathrm{if}~\pi_{2}(\omega^{\prime})=1~\mathrm{then}~\top~\mathrm{else}~\bot;\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega^{\prime},\pi_{1},X)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{observe}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega,\mu,E)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &F:=(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(D))^{-1}(\top);\\
&\mathrm{score}(\mu|_{E}(F));\\
&\mathrm{ret}((\Omega,\mu,E\cap F),\mathrm{id},\star)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\,\rrparenthesis_{E}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &(\Omega^{\prime},f,x)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega^{\prime},f,\_\mapsto x)\end{aligned}\right)

A.6.2.
Sampling sublanguage

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}:{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)

\displaystyle:\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\times(\Omega\to\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket)\to\mathbf{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{ret}}~{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{let}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{be}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{in}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &(\Omega_{1},f_{1},x)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)\\
&(\Omega_{2},f_{2},y)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega_{1})(\gamma[{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}x}\mapsto x],D\circ f_{1})\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega_{2},f_{1}\circ f_{2},y)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{if}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{then}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{else}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{3}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{if}~{\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)}\\
&\mathrm{then}~{\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)}\\
&\mathrm{else}~{\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{3}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{flip}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &x\leftarrow\overline{\operatorname{flip}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))}\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{uniform}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &x\leftarrow\overline{\operatorname{uniform}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma),\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))}\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{poisson}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &x\leftarrow\overline{\operatorname{poisson}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))}\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{flip}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &w:=\operatorname{flip}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\left(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\right)\\
&\mathrm{score}(w);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},\star)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{uniform}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &w:=\operatorname{uniform}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma),\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\left(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\right)\\
&\mathrm{score}(w);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},\star)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathsf{obs}(}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0},}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{poisson}}~{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0})}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &w:=\operatorname{poisson}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{1}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma))\left(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)\right)\\
&\mathrm{score}(w);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(\Omega,\mathrm{id},\star)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),f,X)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega)(\gamma,D)\\
&x\leftarrow\big(\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};~\mathrm{ret}(X(\omega^{\prime}))\big)\\
&\mathrm{ret}((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}\cap X^{-1}(x)),f,x)\end{aligned}\right)

A.7.
Soundness

Definition A.12.

Two computations
\mu,\nu:\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}A

are
equal as importance samplers
,
written
\mu\simeq\nu
,
if for all bounded integrable
k:A\to\mathbb{R}

it holds that
\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,x)\sim\mu}[a\cdot k(x)]=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(b,y)\sim\mu}[b\cdot k(y)]
.

Lemma A.13.

The equivalence relation
\simeq
is a congruence
for the monad
\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}
:
if
\mu\simeq\nu:\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}A

and
f,g:A\to\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}B

with
f(x)\simeq g(x)

for all
x
in
A

then
(x\leftarrow\mu;f(x))\simeq(x\leftarrow\nu;g(x))
.

Proof.

If
k:B\to\mathbb{R}
bounded integrable
then

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(c,y)\sim(x\leftarrow\mu;f(x))}[ck(y)]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,x)\sim\mu}\left[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(b,y)\sim f(x)}[a\cdot b\cdot k(y)]\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,x)\sim\mu}\left[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(b,y)\sim g(x)}[a\cdot b\cdot k(y)]\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,x)\sim\nu}\left[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(b,y)\sim g(x)}[a\cdot b\cdot k(y)]\right]=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(c,y)\sim(x\leftarrow\nu;g(x))}[ck(y)]

where
(1)
follows from
f(x)\simeq g(x)

and
(2)
from
\mu\simeq\nu
, using linearity of expectation throughout
as needed.
∎

Lemma A.14.

If
(\Omega,\mu,E)\in\mathrm{FCPS}

and
F\subseteq\Omega

then
\mu|_{E}|_{F}=\mu|_{E\cap F}
.

Proof.

If
\mu(E\cap F)=0
then both sides are the zero measure.
Otherwise for all
G
have

(\mu|_{E}|_{F})(G)=\mu|_{E}(F\cap G)/\mu|_{E}(F)=\mu(E\cap F\cap G)/\mu(E\cap F)=\mu|_{E\cap F}(G)
.
∎

Lemma A.15.

If
(\Omega,\mu,E)\in\mathrm{FCPS}
then

\left(\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{score}(\mu(E));\\
&\omega\leftarrow\mu|_{E};\\
&\mathrm{ret}~\omega\end{aligned}\right)\simeq\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega\leftarrow\mu;\\
&\mathrm{score}(\mathbf{1}_{\omega\in E});\\
&\mathrm{ret}~\omega\end{aligned}\right)
.

Proof.

For all
k:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,\omega)\sim\text{LHS}}[k(\omega)]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega\sim\mu|_{E}}[\mu(E)k(\omega)]=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mu|_{E}(\omega)\mu(E)k(\omega)=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mu(\omega\cap E)k(\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mathbf{1}_{\omega\in E}\mu(\omega)k(\omega)=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{(a,\omega)\sim\text{RHS}}[a\cdot k(\omega)].

∎

Lemma A.16.

If
(\Omega,\mu,E)\in\mathrm{FCPS}
and
X:\Omega\to A
with
A
finite
then

\left(\begin{aligned} &x\leftarrow\big(\omega\leftarrow\mu;\mathrm{ret}(X\omega)\big);\\
&\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu|_{X^{-1}(x)};\\
&\mathrm{ret}(x,\omega)\end{aligned}\right)=\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu;\\
&\mathrm{ret}(X\omega^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\end{aligned}\right).

Proof.

All distributions involved are discrete, so it suffices to show
LHS and RHS give the same probability to pairs
(a,b)
.

\displaystyle\text{LHS}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mu(\omega)\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mu|_{X^{-1}(X\omega)}(\omega^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{(X\omega,\omega^{\prime})=(a,b)}=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega,X\omega=a,\omega^{\prime}=b}\mu(\omega)\mu|_{X^{-1}(X\omega)}(\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega,X\omega=a}\mu(\omega)\mu|_{X^{-1}(a)}(b)=\mu|_{X^{-1}(a)}(b)\mu(X^{-1}(a))=\mu(X^{-1}(a)\cap b)=\text{RHS}(a,b)

∎

Theorem A.17.

The following hold:

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}

for all

(1)

({{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\mathsf{ret}}~{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}A}})

Let
F
be the subset
(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket(\Omega,\mu,E)\circ D)^{-1}(\top)
of

\mathrm{score}
does not effect the FCPS and we commute with
\mu|_{E}
,

\displaystyle\overset{\textrm{comm}}{=}\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega\leftarrow\mu|_{E};\\
&\mathrm{score}(\overline{\nu}(\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e_{o}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma)));\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D\omega,\star)\end{aligned}\right)=\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega\leftarrow\mu|_{E};\\
&x\leftarrow\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,D\omega);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D\omega,x)\end{aligned}\right)

({{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\Gamma};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\Delta}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}:{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}})

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),f,x)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega,\mu,E)(\gamma,D);\\
&\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D(f(\omega^{\prime})),x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),f,x)\leftarrow\\
&\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),f,X)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega,\mu,E)(\gamma,D)\\
&x\leftarrow\big(\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};~\mathrm{ret}(X\omega^{\prime})\big)\\
&\mathrm{ret}((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}\cap X^{-1}(x)),x)\end{aligned}\right)\\
&\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D(f(\omega^{\prime})),x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),f,X)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega,\mu,E)(\gamma,D)\\
&x\leftarrow\big(\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};~\mathrm{ret}(X\omega^{\prime})\big)\\
&E^{\prime\prime}:=E^{\prime}\cap X^{-1}(x);\\
&\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime\prime}};\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D(f(\omega^{\prime})),x)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftypeplural~refnum}{app:lem:cond-twice} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{app:lem:cond-nested-integral}}}}{{=}}\left(\begin{aligned} &((\Omega^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},E^{\prime}),X)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\Omega,\mu,E)(\gamma,D)\\
&\omega^{\prime}\leftarrow\mu^{\prime}|_{E^{\prime}};\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D(f(\omega^{\prime})),X\omega^{\prime})\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm IH}}{{\simeq}}\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega\leftarrow\mu|_{E};\\
&x\leftarrow\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,D\omega);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D\omega,x)\end{aligned}\right)=\left(\begin{aligned} &\omega\leftarrow\mu|_{E};\\
&x\leftarrow\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}M}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\gamma,D\omega);\\
&\mathrm{ret}(D\omega,x)\end{aligned}\right)

∎

Definition A.18.

For a closed program
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\boldsymbol{\cdot}};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}:{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}
,
let
\mathrm{eval}_{\mathcal{L}}(e)
be the computation

\left(\begin{aligned} &(\_,\_,x)\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\mathrm{emp})(\emptyset,\emptyset);\\
&\mathrm{ret}~x\end{aligned}\right):\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}{\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket}

where
\emptyset
denotes the empty substitution.
Let
\mathrm{eval}_{\mathcal{H}}(e)
be the computation

\mathcal{H}\!\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket\!(\emptyset,\emptyset):\mathrm{Dist}_{\rm w}{\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket}
.

Theorem A.19 (
Cont
soundness).

If
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\cdot};{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}\cdot}\vdash_{\rm c}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e}:{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\tau}

then

\mathrm{eval}_{\mathcal{L}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e})\simeq\mathrm{eval}_{\mathcal{H}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}e})
.

A.17
. ∎

Appendix B
Comparison with Nested Inference

It is interesting to contemplate the relationship between the nested inference
approach and
MultiPPL
. A crisp comparison — for instance, a formal
expressivity result establishing that it is not possible to represent our
multi-language interoperation using nested inference — is difficult, due to
(1) the large variety of different approaches to nested inference, and (2) the
fact that such expressivity results are very hard even for very restricted
languages, let alone rich general-purpose probabilistic programming languages.
It would be very interesting to investigate the relative expressivity of
multi-language interoperation and nested inference, but
such an investigation is beyond the scope of this paper.

At the very least, what we can say is that
MultiPPL
’s low-level denotational
semantics, and hence also its inference strategy, is markedly different from the
standard measure-theoretic semantics of nested inference, such as

Staton (2017)
’s model of nested queries.
In
Staton (2017)
, probabilistic computations denote measure-theoretic
kernels. The computation
\mathsf{normalize}(t)

represents a nested query: its
takes in a probabilistic
computation
t
of type
A

and produces a deterministic computation
that can yield one of three possible outcomes:

(1)

a tuple
(1,(e,d))
consisting of a normalizing constant

e
and a distribution
d
over elements of type
A
,

(2)

a tuple
(2,())
signalling that the normalizing constant
was zero,

(3)

or a tuple
(3,())
signalling that the normalizing constant
was infinity.

Soundness of nested inference is then justified by the
following equational reasoning principle, reproduced here from

Staton (2017)
:

(7)

\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003ptt\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket\hskip 0.50003pt\begin{aligned} \mathsf{case~normalize}(t)~\mathsf{of}~&(1,(e,d))\Rightarrow\mathsf{score}(e);\mathsf{sample}(d)\\
|~&(2,())\Rightarrow\mathsf{score}(0);t\\
|~&(3,())\Rightarrow t\end{aligned}\hskip 0.50003pt\right\rrbracket

Disregarding the edge cases where the normalizing constant
is zero or infinity,

Eq.

7
says
that running a probabilistic computation
t

is the same as (1) computing a representation of
the distribution of
t
, either by exact inference
or by “freezing a simulation” and examining “a histogram that has been built”
in the words of
Staton (2017)
,
and then (2) resampling from this distribution
and scoring by the normalizing constant.

There are a number of points that prevent this model of nested inference
from being applied directly to justify correctness of
MultiPPL
, which
may help to clarify the difference between nested inference and
MultiPPL
’s
inference interoperation:

•

It is about a kernel-based model where programs take deterministic values
as input, but MultiPPL’s

Disc
programs take random variables as input. This
is an important difference: because

Disc
programs take random variables as
input, a

Disc
program that simply makes use of a free variable in context
produces a random variable, not a fixed deterministic value. In contrast, such
a program always denotes a deterministic point mass under a kernel-based
semantics.

•

MultiPPL
’s semantics of

Disc
programs is stateful, and this is
necessary to model how exact inference works in our implementation.
Contrastingly, the kernel-based model of
Staton (2017)
is not
stateful in this way, and so would not have been sufficient for establishing
our main soundness theorem.

•

Because the model of
Staton (2017)
is not stateful,
it can’t account for the stateful updates to the probability space that
MultiPPL

performs in order to ensure sample consistency.

•

Finally,
Eq.

7
suggests to use importance reweighting via

\mathsf{score}
to ensure sound
nesting of exact inference within an approximate-inference context, by properly
taking the normalizing constant produced by the nested query into account.
This is quite different from how
MultiPPL
’s
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}-}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}
boundary form handles
the nesting of

Disc
subterms in

Cont
contexts — as shown in
Fig.

10
,
the low-level semantics of
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\llparenthesis\,}{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.3672,0.9414}-}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\,\rrparenthesis_{S}}
does not perform importance reweighting.
Instead, importance reweighting occurs in the semantics of

Disc
-observe
statements. Thus
Eq.

7
does not explain
MultiPPL
’s importance reweighting scheme.

Together, these points show that multi-language inference
is distinct enough from nested inference that the standard measure-theoretic
model of nested queries from
Staton (2017)

cannot be used directly to justify key aspects of
MultiPPL
’s inference strategy,
such as the need for sample consistency and when importance reweighting is performed.
Though
Staton (2017)
is just one approach to modelling nested inference,
it being a relatively well-established approach suggests that there are indeed
fundamental differences between nested inference and
MultiPPL
’s multilanguage inference.
